If there are\nuncommitted changes, or the range diff is empty, also run `git diff HEAD` and\ninclude the working-tree changes in scope — the review often runs before the\ncommit. If a PR number, branch name, or file path was passed as an argument,\nreview that target instead. Treat this diff as the review scope.\n\nReview the diff as a careful senior engineer would: read every hunk, open the surrounding files for context as needed (Read, Grep, git log/blame/show), and hunt for correctness issues — wrong or inverted conditions, off-by-one, null/undefined dereference, missing `await`, dropped error handling, removed guards or validations, broken callers of changed functions, races. Reach for it the moment a ruff, ty or other linter bump turns a green gate red — before fixing anything and before reaching for a suppression — and whenever a Renovate tool-bump PR arrives with a wall of new diagnostics. Newly-enabled rules look exactly like regressions in your own change — one measured bump took a tree from 2 violations to 138 without a line of code changing.\n- lock-image: Regenerate the devcontainer IMAGE lockfiles (`.devcontainer/mise-system.lock` + `mise-runtime.lock`) via `mise run lock-image`. Use whenever a change to `.devcontainer/mise-system.toml`, `.devcontainer/mise-runtime.toml` or `.config/mise/conf.d/shared.toml` needs its locks refreshed, when `mise run lint`'s `mise_lock_integrity` step or CI reports the image locks stale or short, or when you are about to transcribe the recipe out of `.github/actions/lock-refresh/action.yml` by hand. Reach for it BEFORE hand-rolling `mise lock` against a staged config — regenerating these two files on macOS truncates them silently, and the tool count does not move, so the damage reads as success.\n- mcp2cli: Invoke MCP servers as one-shot CLI commands (no native registration, no schema injection into Claude's context). Use whenever a research or tool-use task wants an MCP server's capabilities without paying the per-conversation context tax of `claude mcp add`.\n- memory-index-curation: Use when curating the auto-memory index (MEMORY.md) — trimming a bloated hook, deleting a stale memory, or reacting to a size warning. Shortening a hook silently destroys any fact that lives only there, so the order is verify → migrate/correct → THEN shorten. Run `mise run memory-index` first.\n- pr-workflow: Ship, automerge and land PRs via `mise run ship` / `mise run automerge -- <PR#>` / `mise run land -- <PR#>` — the full gated loop from committed work to merged-and-locally-validated main. Use whenever committing work that should become a PR, when asked to merge a green PR, when a bot-opened PR (Renovate / the refresh bot / a dependency bump) needs merging, or when validating that the ship/land wiring is intact. Never hand-roll commit→push→PR→merge sequences.\n- reap: Clear a pile of wedged processes safely via `mise run reap` — a dry-run-by-default reaper with ancestor-chain protection, an age floor, and TERM-then-KILL escalation. Reach for it whenever a host has accumulated stuck or duplicated processes (hung `git`, `fnox`, shims, orphaned workers), when load, PID or memory pressure needs a bulk cleanup, and above all before hand-rolling a `pkill`, a `ps | grep | awk | xargs kill`, or a throwaway kill script — that improvisation nearly killed a session's own shell and is why this exists. Also use it to measure a pile before deciding anything, since a dry run signals nothing and prints the full plan.\n- session-review: Find what a session did BY HAND that should have become a skill, mise task or python module, via `mise run session-review`. Use at the end of a working session, during `/clear-prep` or handoff writing, when a task felt like a slog and you want to know whether it was one, or when deciding what automation to build next. Two disjoint lanes — a transcript mine for recurring command shapes and a narrative pass over the notepad and handoff for reasoning sinks that leave no repeated command. Run it before writing the handoff, while the session's own notes are still in front of you.\n- token-check: Prove a candidate contract token binds exactly one site BEFORE writing the contract, via `mise run token-check -- <file> \"<token>\"...`. Use whenever adding or editing a `[[suite]]` in `python/verification/suites.toml`, choosing `per_path_tokens` for a new gate, or reacting to a `contract_token_uniqueness` / `token-audit` finding. Run it while picking the tokens, not after — a token matching more than once can be satisfied by a stand-in, so the contract silently asserts less than it claims, and that is how a deleted registration stayed green.\n- tool-currency-check: Use when auditing whether the repo's pinned tools are current AND whether any hand-rolled custom code is now superseded by a native tool feature. {"parentUuid":"582ed421-badb-4179-ab4d-dfc77517de4a","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"568a3094-ff17-4144-8041-81815078475a","agentId":"ae619a3115cc0fa20","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012R22hczEoXEiayGz6GdKK3","type":"tool_result","content":"diff --git a/python/src/dotfiles_setup/devcontainer_names.py b/python/src/dotfiles_setup/devcontainer_names.py\nnew file mode 100644\nindex 0000000..3d50355\n--- /dev/null\n+++ b/python/src/dotfiles_setup/devcontainer_names.py\n@@ -0,0 +1,465 @@\n+# Copyright (c) 2026 Raymond Manaloto\n+\"\"\"Architecture-scoped devcontainer resource names (#677).\n+\n+Why the architecture has to be in the name\n+------------------------------------------\n+\n+The home volume carries architecture-specific *compiled output*:\n+``~/.local/share/mise/installs``, ``~/.cargo`` and ``~/.rustup`` all live on it.\n+Mount one volume into an amd64 container and then into an arm64 one and docker\n+reports nothing — a named volume is created on first mount and reused\n+thereafter, by design. What you get instead is a home directory holding\n+binaries of two architectures, surfacing as ``exec format error`` or a wrong\n+resolve far from its cause. The same shape as #698: **absence and wrongness\n+that present as success.**\n+\n+So the arch is a *name* component, not a runtime check. Two architectures\n+become two containers, two volumes and two ports, and the operating system's\n+own uniqueness rules do the enforcing.\n+\n+Where each component comes from\n+-------------------------------\n+\n+* the **workspace hash** — an 8-char SHA-256 prefix of the absolute workspace\n+  path, so sibling clones on one host never share resources. This is a port of\n+  the retired ``scripts/workspace-hash.sh`` and reproduces it byte-for-byte\n+  (:func:`workspace_hash`); a different digest would rename every deployed\n+  volume, and a renamed volume reads as an empty home, not as an error.\n+* the **architecture word** — resolved through\n+  :mod:`dotfiles_setup.platform_target`, never written as a literal. The\n+  ``no_platform_literals`` gate rejects the literal form anyway (#673).\n+* the **SSH port** — an explicit ``DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT`` if set, else derived\n+  from workspace *and* arch (:func:`ssh_port`), so a second working directory\n+  and a second architecture each get a distinct port with no configuration.\n+\n+The port is in the container name and deliberately **not** in the volume name:\n+changing a port must not orphan a home directory (C10/C11/C12).\n+\"\"\"\n+\n+from __future__ import annotations\n+\n+import hashlib\n+import logging\n+import os\n+import shlex\n+import subprocess\n+import sys\n+from dataclasses import dataclass\n+from pathlib import Path\n+\n+from dotfiles_setup.platform_target import (\n+    PLATFORM_ENV_VAR,\n+    platform_arch,\n+    resolve_platform,\n+)\n+\n+logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n+\n+__all__ = [\n+    \"ARCH_ENV_VAR\",\n+    \"HOME_VOLUME_ENV_VAR\",\n+    \"NAME_ENV_VAR\",\n+    \"NAME_FIELDS\",\n+    \"RESOURCE_PREFIX\",\n+    \"SSH_PORT_BASE\",\n+    \"SSH_PORT_ENV_VAR\",\n+    \"SSH_PORT_SPAN\",\n+    \"WORKSPACE_HASH_ENV_VAR\",\n+    \"DevcontainerNames\",\n+    \"HomeVolumeMigration\",\n+    \"devcontainer_env_main\",\n+    \"devcontainer_name_main\",\n+    \"migrate_home_volume_main\",\n+    \"migration_platform_refusal\",\n+    \"name_field\",\n+    \"names_env\",\n+    \"plan_home_volume_migration\",\n+    \"render_plan\",\n+    \"resolve_names\",\n+    \"ssh_port\",\n+    \"workspace_hash\",\n+]\n+\n+#: Every resource this repo creates starts with it, so `ps`/`prune` can scope.\n+RESOURCE_PREFIX = \"dotfiles\"\n+\n+WORKSPACE_HASH_ENV_VAR = \"DEVCONTAINER_WORKSPACE_HASH\"\n+ARCH_ENV_VAR = \"DEVCONTAINER_ARCH\"\n+NAME_ENV_VAR = \"DEVCONTAINER_NAME\"\n+HOME_VOLUME_ENV_VAR = \"DEVCONTAINER_HOME_VOLUME\"\n+SSH_PORT_ENV_VAR = \"DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT\"\n+\n+#: The derived-port window. It opens above the well-known/registered churn and\n+#: closes below 49152, where macOS starts handing out ephemeral ports to\n+#: anonymous binds — a derived port inside that range would be stolen\n+#: intermittently, and R1 would fail on a timer rather than on a defect.\n+SSH_PORT_BASE = 20000\n+SSH_PORT_SPAN = 10000\n+\n+#: Length of the workspace hash. Fixed by the deployed volume names — see\n+#: :func:`workspace_hash`.\n+_HASH_CHARS = 8\n+\n+#: What ``dotfiles-setup devcontainer name <field>`` can print.\n+NAME_FIELDS = (\"container\", \"volume\", \"legacy-volume\", \"port\", \"hash\", \"arch\")\n+\n+\n+def workspace_hash(workspace: str | Path) -> str:\n+    \"\"\"The 8-char SHA-256 prefix of ``workspace``'s absolute path.\n+\n+    Byte-compatible with the retired ``scripts/workspace-hash.sh``\n+    (``printf '%s' \"$PWD\" | sha256sum | cut -c1-8``) — asserted by\n+    ``tests/test_devcontainer_names.py`` against both an independent\n+    re-derivation and frozen goldens, because a drifting digest renames every\n+    existing volume and a renamed volume looks like an empty home.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    resolved = str(Path(workspace).resolve())\n+    return hashlib.sha256(resolved.encode()).hexdigest()[:_HASH_CHARS]\n+\n+\n+def ssh_port(\n+    workspace: str | Path,\n+    arch: str,\n+    *,\n+    override: str | int | None = None,\n+) -> int:\n+    \"\"\"The host-side SSH port for this workspace and architecture.\n+\n+    ``override`` wins when it holds a value — that is ``DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT``,\n+    the documented per-clone escape hatch. A *blank* override falls through to\n+    derivation on purpose: mise renders an unset variable as the empty string\n+    rather than omitting it, so \"unset\" reaches Python as ``\"\"``.\n+\n+    An unparsable override raises rather than falling back, because silently\n+    deriving a port from a typo'd pin is how a container comes up on a port\n+    nobody is ssh-ing to.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    if override is not None and str(override).strip():\n+        text = str(override).strip()\n+        try:\n+            return int(text)\n+        except ValueError:\n+            msg = (\n+                f\"{SSH_PORT_ENV_VAR}={text!r} is not a port number — unset it to \"\n+                f\"derive one from the workspace and architecture\"\n+            )\n+            raise ValueError(msg) from None\n+    seed = f\"{Path(workspace).resolve()}\\0{arch}\".encode()\n+    offset = int(hashlib.sha256(seed).hexdigest()[:_HASH_CHARS], 16) % SSH_PORT_SPAN\n+    return SSH_PORT_BASE + offset\n+\n+\n+@dataclass(frozen=True)\n+class DevcontainerNames:\n+    \"\"\"Every docker resource name one workspace+architecture pair owns.\"\"\"\n+\n+    workspace: str\n+    basename: str\n+    user: str\n+    arch: str\n+    hash: str\n+    ssh_port: int\n+\n+    @property\n+    def _stem(self) -> str:\n+        return f\"{RESOURCE_PREFIX}-{self.basename}-{self.user}-{self.hash}\"\n+\n+    @property\n+    def container(self) -> str:\n+        \"\"\"``dotfiles-<basename>-<user>-<hash>-<arch>-<port>``.\"\"\"\n+        return f\"{self._stem}-{self.arch}-{self.ssh_port}\"\n+\n+    @property\n+    def home_volume(self) -> str:\n+        \"\"\"``dotfiles-<basename>-<user>-<hash>-<arch>-home`` — no port (C10-C12).\"\"\"\n+        return f\"{self._stem}-{self.arch}-home\"\n+\n+    @property\n+    def legacy_home_volume(self) -> str:\n+        \"\"\"The pre-#677 single-architecture home volume: the migration source.\"\"\"\n+        return f\"{self._stem}-home\"\n+\n+\n+def resolve_names(\n+    *,\n+    workspace: str | Path | None = None,\n+    user: str | None = None,\n+    platform: str | None = None,\n+    port_override: str | int | None = None,\n+    env: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n+) -> DevcontainerNames:\n+    \"\"\"Resolve every name for one workspace+architecture.\n+\n+    Each argument defaults to the ambient reality the devloop runs in: the\n+    current directory, ``$USER``, and the one platform parameter (#673).\n+    \"\"\"\n+    environ = dict(os.environ) if env is None else env\n+    source = workspace if workspace is not None else Path.cwd()\n+    resolved_workspace = Path(source).resolve()\n+    resolved_user = user if user is not None else environ.get(\"USER\", \"\")\n+    arch = platform_arch(resolve_platform(platform, env=environ))\n+    override = (\n+        port_override if port_override is not None else environ.get(SSH_PORT_ENV_VAR)\n+    )\n+    return DevcontainerNames(\n+        workspace=str(resolved_workspace),\n+        basename=resolved_workspace.name,\n+        user=resolved_user,\n+        arch=arch,\n+        hash=workspace_hash(resolved_workspace),\n+        ssh_port=ssh_port(resolved_workspace, arch, override=override),\n+    )\n+\n+\n+def names_env(names: DevcontainerNames) -> dict[str, str]:\n+    \"\"\"The ``localEnv`` substitutions ``devcontainer.json`` reads.\n+\n+    ``devcontainer.json`` composes nothing itself: it names whole values. That\n+    is what keeps the arch out of a JSON string literal, where\n+    ``no_platform_literals`` could not see it and a reviewer would not either.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    return {\n+        WORKSPACE_HASH_ENV_VAR: names.hash,\n+        ARCH_ENV_VAR: names.arch,\n+        NAME_ENV_VAR: names.container,\n+        HOME_VOLUME_ENV_VAR: names.home_volume,\n+        SSH_PORT_ENV_VAR: str(names.ssh_port),\n+    }\n+\n+\n+def name_field(field: str, names: DevcontainerNames | None = None) -> str:\n+    \"\"\"One resolved name, addressed by name.\n+\n+    ``names`` defaults to this workspace's — pass one from :func:`resolve_names`\n+    to ask about a workspace or architecture that is not the one you are in.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    if names is None:\n+        names = resolve_names()\n+    fields = {\n+        \"container\": names.container,\n+        \"volume\": names.home_volume,\n+        \"legacy-volume\": names.legacy_home_volume,\n+        \"port\": str(names.ssh_port),\n+        \"hash\": names.hash,\n+        \"arch\": names.arch,\n+    }\n+    if field not in fields:\n+        msg = (\n+            f\"unknown devcontainer name field {field!r}: expected one of {NAME_FIELDS}\"\n+        )\n+        raise ValueError(msg)\n+    return fields[field]\n+\n+\n+@dataclass(frozen=True)\n+class HomeVolumeMigration:\n+    \"\"\"What (if anything) to do about a pre-#677 home volume.\"\"\"\n+\n+    action: str  # \"copy\" | \"already-migrated\" | \"nothing-to-migrate\"\n+    source: str\n+    target: str\n+    commands: tuple[tuple[str, ...], ...]\n+    reason: str\n+\n+\n+#: The copier. `busybox` is a ~4MB multi-arch image whose `cp -a` preserves\n+#: ownership, modes and symlinks; the alternative (`tar` piped through the\n+#: host) would re-encode the whole home through a shell pipeline for no gain.\n+_MIGRATION_IMAGE = \"busybox:stable\"\n+\n+\n+def plan_home_volume_migration(\n+    names: DevcontainerNames,\n+    *,\n+    existing_volumes: tuple[str, ...],\n+    target_populated: bool = False,\n+) -> HomeVolumeMigration:\n+    \"\"\"Plan the one-shot copy from the pre-#677 home volume into this one.\n+\n+    Pure: it decides from facts the caller measured, so the decision is\n+    testable without a docker daemon.\n+\n+    Three cases, and the third is the one that matters. A target volume that\n+    *exists but is empty* is precisely what an interrupted first attempt leaves\n+    behind — docker created it when the copy container started and the copy\n+    never finished. Treating \"exists\" as \"done\" would strand a half-migrated\n+    home forever, so emptiness, not existence, is the signal (#677 AC:\n+    \"an interrupted first creation leaves a state the next attempt can recover\n+    from\").\n+\n+    The source is never deleted here. Until the user has run the new container\n+    and is satisfied, the legacy volume is the only copy of their home;\n+    ``mise run prune`` is where removal belongs.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    if names.legacy_home_volume not in existing_volumes:\n+        return HomeVolumeMigration(\n+            action=\"nothing-to-migrate\",\n+            source=names.legacy_home_volume,\n+            target=names.home_volume,\n+            commands=(),\n+            reason=(\n+                f\"no pre-#677 volume {names.legacy_home_volume!r} on this host — \"\n+                f\"{names.home_volume!r} will be created empty on first use\"\n+            ),\n+        )\n+    if target_populated:\n+        return HomeVolumeMigration(\n+            action=\"already-migrated\",\n+            source=names.legacy_home_volume,\n+            target=names.home_volume,\n+            commands=(),\n+            reason=(\n+                f\"{names.home_volume!r} already holds a home directory — copying \"\n+                f\"over it would destroy live state\"\n+            ),\n+        )\n+    return HomeVolumeMigration(\n+        action=\"copy\",\n+        source=names.legacy_home_volume,\n+        target=names.home_volume,\n+        commands=(\n+            (\"docker\", \"volume\", \"create\", names.home_volume),\n+            (\n+                \"docker\",\n+                \"run\",\n+                \"--rm\",\n+                \"-v\",\n+                f\"{names.legacy_home_volume}:/from:ro\",\n+                \"-v\",\n+                f\"{names.home_volume}:/to\",\n+                _MIGRATION_IMAGE,\n+                \"sh\",\n+                \"-c\",\n+                \"cp -a /from/. /to/\",\n+            ),\n+        ),\n+        reason=(\n+            f\"copying {names.legacy_home_volume!r} into {names.home_volume!r}; \"\n+            f\"the source is left intact — remove it with `mise run prune` once \"\n+            f\"the new container is known good\"\n+        ),\n+    )\n+\n+\n+def render_plan(plan: HomeVolumeMigration) -> str:\n+    \"\"\"The dry-run rendering: the reason plus every command, one per line.\"\"\"\n+    lines = [f\"home-volume migration: {plan.action} — {plan.reason}\"]\n+    lines.extend(f\"  $ {shlex.join(cmd)}\" for cmd in plan.commands)\n+    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n+\n+\n+def _docker_volumes() -> tuple[str, ...]:\n+    \"\"\"Every named volume on this host.\"\"\"\n+    proc = subprocess.run(\n+        [\"docker\", \"volume\", \"ls\", \"--format\", \"{{.Name}}\"],\n+        capture_output=True,\n+        text=True,\n+        check=True,\n+    )\n+    return tuple(line for line in proc.stdout.splitlines() if line.strip())\n+\n+\n+def _volume_is_populated(volume: str) -> bool:\n+    \"\"\"True when ``volume`` already holds at least one entry.\n+\n+    Only ever called for a volume already known to exist, because mounting a\n+    missing one would *create* it — and an empty volume created by the probe\n+    would then be indistinguishable from an interrupted migration.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    proc = subprocess.run(\n+        [\n+            \"docker\",\n+            \"run\",\n+            \"--rm\",\n+            \"-v\",\n+            f\"{volume}:/probe:ro\",\n+            _MIGRATION_IMAGE,\n+            \"sh\",\n+            \"-c\",\n+            \"ls -A /probe\",\n+        ],\n+        capture_output=True,\n+        text=True,\n+        check=True,\n+    )\n+    return bool(proc.stdout.strip())\n+\n+\n+def migration_platform_refusal(\n+    platform: str | None, env: dict[str, str] | None = None\n+) -> str | None:\n+    \"\"\"Why this migration must not guess an architecture, or ``None`` if it need not.\n+\n+    The pre-#677 volume carries **no architecture in its name**, so nothing on\n+    the host records which architecture built its contents. Only the platform\n+    the caller was targeting does — and :func:`resolve_platform` falls back to\n+    the *host's* native triple when nothing pins it. Measured on this repo:\n+    the same command resolves ``amd64`` under ``mise run`` (which supplies the\n+    repo pin) and ``arm64`` from a bare shell on an M-series Mac. A copy is\n+    not a probe you can re-run for free, so an unpinned invocation refuses\n+    rather than picking one.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    if platform:\n+        return None\n+    environ = os.environ if env is None else env\n+    if environ.get(PLATFORM_ENV_VAR, \"\").strip():\n+        return None\n+    return (\n+        f\"refusing to guess an architecture: {PLATFORM_ENV_VAR} is unset, so the \"\n+        f\"target volume name would come from this HOST's architecture, not from \"\n+        f\"the one that built the home directory being copied. Run \"\n+        f\"`mise run migrate-home-volume` (which supplies the repo's pin) or pass \"\n+        f\"--platform explicitly\"\n+    )\n+\n+\n+def migrate_home_volume_main(*, apply: bool, platform: str | None = None) -> int:\n+    \"\"\"CLI entry: copy a pre-#677 home volume into the arch-scoped one.\n+\n+    Dry-run by default. ``--apply`` is required to touch anything, following\n+    ``reap``'s precedent (#653): a bulk operation whose bare invocation mutates\n+    is one nobody can safely use to *look*.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    refusal = migration_platform_refusal(platform)\n+    if refusal is not None:\n+        logger.error(\"migrate-home: %s\", refusal)\n+        return 2\n+    names = resolve_names(platform=platform)\n+    volumes = _docker_volumes()\n+    target_exists = names.home_volume in volumes\n+    populated = _volume_is_populated(names.home_volume) if target_exists else False\n+    plan = plan_home_volume_migration(\n+        names, existing_volumes=volumes, target_populated=populated\n+    )\n+    sys.stdout.write(f\"{render_plan(plan)}\\n\")\n+    if not plan.commands:\n+        return 0\n+    if not apply:\n+        sys.stdout.write(\"(dry run — pass --apply to execute)\\n\")\n+        return 0\n+    for cmd in plan.commands:\n+        sys.stdout.write(f\"==> {shlex.join(cmd)}\\n\")\n+        subprocess.run(cmd, check=True)\n+    sys.stdout.write(\n+        f\"OK: {names.legacy_home_volume} copied into {names.home_volume}; the \"\n+        f\"source is untouched\\n\"\n+    )\n+    return 0\n+\n+\n+def devcontainer_env_main() -> int:\n+    \"\"\"CLI entry: print shell ``KEY=value`` exports for the devloop tasks.\n+\n+    Consumed as ``eval \"$(… devcontainer env)\"``. Values are shell-quoted, and\n+    every one of them is derived here rather than in the task body — the whole\n+    point of [[zero-bash-logic]] is that this decision has exactly one home.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    for key, value in names_env(resolve_names()).items():\n+        sys.stdout.write(f\"export {key}={shlex.quote(value)}\\n\")\n+    return 0\n+\n+\n+def devcontainer_name_main(field: str) -> int:\n+    \"\"\"CLI entry: print one resolved devcontainer resource name.\"\"\"\n+    sys.stdout.write(f\"{name_field(field, resolve_names())}\\n\")\n+    return 0","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"3466ef45-e33e-4ae0-9a2e-645d27473ade","timestamp":"2026-08-10T11:33:29.712Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"582ed421-badb-4179-ab4d-dfc77517de4a","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"eeaf6a41-3473-4c9e-a681-cca17051c6ab","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"feat/677-arch-scoped-devcontainer-names"}

NOTE: the feature's `port`/`username`/`startNow`\n //        options are silently dropped (see research report); sshd\n //        listens on its hardcoded **2222** internally. External port is\n-//        `${DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT}` (default 4444) via `appPort`.\n+//        `${DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT}` via `appPort` — derived per\n+//        workspace+architecture (#677), or pinned in mise.local.toml.\n //      - `postCreateCommand` copies the host user's authorized keys\n //        (pre-written by `dotfiles-setup docker initialize-host` on the\n //        host from `ssh-add -L`) into `~/.ssh/authorized_keys` so the\n@@ -75,7 +76,12 @@\n // ===============================================================\n {\n   \"$schema\": \"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/devcontainers/spec/main/schemas/devContainer.schema.json\",\n-  \"name\": \"dotfiles-${localWorkspaceFolderBasename}-${localEnv:USER}-${localEnv:DEVCONTAINER_WORKSPACE_HASH}-${localEnv:DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT}\",\n+  // #677: the container/volume/port names are RESOLVED, never composed here.\n+  // `mise run up` evals `dotfiles-setup devcontainer env`, which derives all\n+  // of them — including the architecture word, which must come from\n+  // platform_target and never from a literal (`no_platform_literals`). A JSON\n+  // string is also the one place a reviewer cannot see an arch is missing.\n+  \"name\": \"${localEnv:DEVCONTAINER_NAME}\",\n   \"build\": {\n     \"dockerfile\": \"Dockerfile.host-user\",\n     // #673: the platform is ONE parameter — mise.toml's global\n@@ -92,7 +98,7 @@\n   },\n   \"runArgs\": [\n     \"--name\",\n-    \"dotfiles-${localWorkspaceFolderBasename}-${localEnv:USER}-${localEnv:DEVCONTAINER_WORKSPACE_HASH}-${localEnv:DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT}\",\n+    \"${localEnv:DEVCONTAINER_NAME}\",\n     \"--env-file\",\n     \"${localEnv:HOME}/.local/state/dotfiles/doppler.env\",\n     \"--platform=${localEnv:DOTFILES_PLATFORM}\",\n@@ -103,7 +109,11 @@\n   \"mounts\": [\n     \"source=${localEnv:HOME}/.local/state/dotfiles,target=/tmp/dotfiles-host-state,type=bind\",\n     \"source=/run/host-services/ssh-auth.sock,target=/run/host-services/ssh-auth.sock,type=bind\",\n-    \"source=dotfiles-${localWorkspaceFolderBasename}-${localEnv:USER}-${localEnv:DEVCONTAINER_WORKSPACE_HASH}-home,target=/home/${localEnv:USER},type=volume\",\n+    // Architecture-scoped (#677). The home volume carries compiled output —\n+    // ~/.local/share/mise/installs, ~/.cargo, ~/.rustup — so sharing one\n+    // across architectures interleaves binaries silently: docker reuses a\n+    // named volume on mount and reports nothing.\n+    \"source=${localEnv:DEVCONTAINER_HOME_VOLUME},target=/home/${localEnv:USER},type=volume\",\n   ],\n   \"containerEnv\": {\n     // ─ Platform hints ────────────────────────────────────────────────\ndiff --git a/mise.local.toml.example b/mise.local.toml.example\nindex 0b3026e..963b83b 100644\n--- a/mise.local.toml.example\n+++ b/mise.local.toml.example\n@@ -15,9 +15,15 @@\n # Overridable vars (consumed by the up / dev-rebuild / verify-* tasks):\n #\n #   - DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT — host-side port for the inbound ssh shell\n-#     (Requirement R1). Default 4444. Bump on port collision; volume names do\n-#     NOT include the port (C10/C11/C12), so you keep your home volume when\n-#     recovering.\n+#     (Requirement R1). UNSET is now the good default: the port is DERIVED\n+#     from the workspace path and the architecture (#677), so a second clone\n+#     and a second architecture each get a distinct one with no configuration.\n+#     `mise run ssh-port` prints it. Pin a value here only if you want a\n+#     stable, memorable port for THIS clone (e.g. an IDE ssh config, an\n+#     entry in ~/.ssh/config). Volume names still do NOT include the port\n+#     (C10/C11/C12), so changing it never costs you the home volume — but it\n+#     does pin BOTH architectures to one port, so unset it before bringing a\n+#     second architecture up in this directory.\n #   - BASE_IMAGE — pin to a specific tag/SHA for reproducible rebuilds.\n #     Default ghcr.io/ray-manaloto/dotfiles-devcontainer:dev.\n #   - DOPPLER_CONFIG — which Doppler config the devcontainer downloads.\n@@ -27,6 +33,6 @@\n # DO NOT check in mise.local.toml; it is gitignored by design.\n \n [env]\n-DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT = \"4444\"\n+# DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT = \"4444\"   # leave unset to derive per workspace+arch\n # BASE_IMAGE = \"ghcr.io/ray-manaloto/dotfiles-devcontainer:<sha>\"\n # DOPPLER_CONFIG = \"dev\"\ndiff --git a/mise.toml b/mise.toml\nindex 2eb90f9..cc28343 100644\n--- a/mise.toml\n+++ b/mise.toml\n@@ -276,15 +276,17 @@ description = \"Bring the devcontainer up via the official @devcontainers/cli (Ma\n # keeps these TASK-scoped (no global leak of BASE_IMAGE, nor of the\n # docker-honoured DOCKER_DEFAULT_PLATFORM, into unrelated subprocesses)\n # unless the user opts in.\n-env = { BASE_IMAGE = \"{{ env.BASE_IMAGE | default(value='ghcr.io/ray-manaloto/dotfiles-devcontainer:dev') }}\", DOCKER_DEFAULT_PLATFORM = \"{{ env.DOTFILES_PLATFORM }}\", DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT = \"{{ env.DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT | default(value='4444') }}\", DOPPLER_PROJECT = \"dotfiles\", DOPPLER_CONFIG = \"{{ env.DOPPLER_CONFIG | default(value='dev_personal') }}\" }\n+env = { BASE_IMAGE = \"{{ env.BASE_IMAGE | default(value='ghcr.io/ray-manaloto/dotfiles-devcontainer:dev') }}\", DOCKER_DEFAULT_PLATFORM = \"{{ env.DOTFILES_PLATFORM }}\", DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT = \"{{ env.DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT | default(value='') }}\", DOPPLER_PROJECT = \"dotfiles\", DOPPLER_CONFIG = \"{{ env.DOPPLER_CONFIG | default(value='dev_personal') }}\" }\n run = '''\n set -euo pipefail\n-# v6 collision guard: compute an 8-char hash of $PWD so sibling clones\n-# of `dotfiles` on the same host get distinct Docker volume + container\n-# names. Single source of truth at scripts/workspace-hash.sh.\n-DEVCONTAINER_WORKSPACE_HASH=\"$(scripts/workspace-hash.sh)\"\n-export DEVCONTAINER_WORKSPACE_HASH\n+# v7 (#677): every devcontainer resource name — container, home volume and\n+# SSH port — is resolved by dotfiles_setup.devcontainer_names and exported\n+# for devcontainer.json's ${localEnv:...} substitution. {"parentUuid":"1bcbb406-3793-42e8-ad1d-6ac3bc184306","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"568a3094-ff17-4144-8041-81815078475a","agentId":"ae619a3115cc0fa20","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018EgCafMyGfunnk45PG8EC9","type":"tool_result","content":"#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# workspace-hash.sh — print an 8-char hash of $PWD to stdout.\n#\n# Used by mise.toml [tasks.up], [tasks.ps], [tasks.prune] to produce a\n# stable per-workspace suffix for Docker container/volume names. Sibling\n# clones of dotfiles/ on the same Mac get distinct hashes so volumes\n# don't collide (Codex v3 finding — see\n# .agent/plans/home-volume-consolidation-draft.md C10/C11/C12).\n#\n# Single source of truth replaces three identical-but-copy-pasted blocks\n# in mise.toml that risked drift on the next refactor.\n#\n# Portable hash detection: sha256sum is Linux default; shasum is macOS\n# default. Try both. Fail loud (no fallback to any other algorithm) if\n# neither is available — the contract is \"8-char SHA-256 prefix\"; using\n# md5 or another digest would change volume names across hosts.\nset -euo pipefail\n\nif command -v sha256sum >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n\tprintf '%s' \"$PWD\" | sha256sum | cut -c1-8\nelif command -v shasum >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n\tprintf '%s' \"$PWD\" | shasum -a 256 | cut -c1-8\nelse\n\techo \"workspace-hash: neither sha256sum nor shasum found — cannot compute workspace hash\" >&2\n\texit 1\nfi","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"9bd1f723-39b0-4ad3-ad09-ea2d673e6079","timestamp":"2026-08-10T11:34:20.550Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"1bcbb406-3793-42e8-ad1d-6ac3bc184306","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"eeaf6a41-3473-4c9e-a681-cca17051c6ab","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"feat/677-arch-scoped-devcontainer-names"}

\"build.devcontainer-names-arch-scoped\"\n+description = \"#677: the container name, the home volume and the SSH port all carry the architecture, so amd64 and arm64 can be up in one working directory without sharing resources. The home volume is the dangerous one — it holds ~/.local/share/mise/installs, ~/.cargo and ~/.rustup, i.e. compiled output — and docker reuses a named volume on mount without a word, so a shared volume interleaves binaries of two architectures and surfaces far from its cause. devcontainer.json therefore NAMES whole values (${localEnv:DEVCONTAINER_NAME} / ${localEnv:DEVCONTAINER_HOME_VOLUME}) instead of composing them from parts: composition in a JSON string is a place an arch can go missing where neither no_platform_literals nor a reviewer would see it. mise.toml binds the resolver at the two sites that would otherwise re-derive a name — R3's container lookup and R1's port.\"\n+category = \"build\"\n+check_type = \"static\"\n+handler = \"require_tokens\"\n+paths = [\".devcontainer/devcontainer.json\", \"mise.toml\"]\n+per_path_tokens = { \".devcontainer/devcontainer.json\" = [\n+    \"\\\"name\\\": \\\"${localEnv:DEVCONTAINER_NAME}\\\"\",\n+    \"\\\"source=${localEnv:DEVCONTAINER_HOME_VOLUME},target=/home/${localEnv:USER},type=volume\\\"\",\n+], \"mise.toml\" = [\n+    \"dotfiles-setup devcontainer name container\",\n+    \"dotfiles-setup devcontainer migrate-home\",\n+] }\n+\n+[[suite]]\n+name = \"build.devcontainer-names-not-recomposed\"\n+description = \"#677 companion: reject re-composing a devcontainer resource name from parts inside devcontainer.json. This is the regression the ticket exists to prevent, and it is invisible by construction — a name assembled from ${localWorkspaceFolderBasename} and a hash simply omits the architecture, and every container still starts. Names are resolved once, in dotfiles_setup.devcontainer_names, and arrive here whole.\"\n+category = \"build\"\n+check_type = \"static\"\n+handler = \"regex_forbid\"\n+paths = [\".devcontainer/devcontainer.json\"]\n+pattern = 'dotfiles-\\$\\{localWorkspaceFolderBasename\\}'\n+\n [[suite]]\n name = \"build.uv-link-mode-not-global\"\n description = \"UV_LINK_MODE=copy must not be a global ENV (breaks runtime)\"\ndiff --git a/scripts/validate-devcontainer-json.sh b/scripts/validate-devcontainer-json.sh\nindex cc8dd2a..25e22d8 100755\n--- a/scripts/validate-devcontainer-json.sh\n+++ b/scripts/validate-devcontainer-json.sh\n@@ -49,9 +49,9 @@ echo \"[validate-devcontainer-json] Layer 2/3: devcontainer read-configuration\"\n # Stub env vars: these are resolved at read time but we don't care about\n # the values — only that substitution succeeds and the JSONC parses.\n parsed=\"$(\n-\tDEVCONTAINER_WORKSPACE_HASH=validate \\\n-\t\tBASE_IMAGE=validate \\\n+\tBASE_IMAGE=validate \\\n \t\tDEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT=4444 \\\n+\t\tDEVCONTAINER_NAME=validate DEVCONTAINER_HOME_VOLUME=validate \\\n \t\tdevcontainer read-configuration --workspace-folder .\n )\"\n if [ -z \"${parsed}\" ]; then","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"b3699dee-1b26-4334-a1db-18003b2c0f57","timestamp":"2026-08-10T11:34:33.367Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"308e3c3d-65db-46c8-ad82-0b69a0dec5b0","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"eeaf6a41-3473-4c9e-a681-cca17051c6ab","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"feat/677-arch-scoped-devcontainer-names"}

Careful editing threads the parameter through the \"\n        \"sites you remembered; this finds the ones you did not, and missing \"\n        \"one yields a container running one architecture while a probe \"\n        \"asserts another\",\n    )\n\n\ndef _add_devcontainer_subcommands(subparsers: _SubParsers) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Register `devcontainer env|name|migrate-home` (#677).\n\n    Its own function so ``_add_honesty_subcommands`` stays under ruff's\n    statement cap — the same reason ``_add_report_parsers`` exists.\n    \"\"\"\n    devcontainer_parser = subparsers.add_parser(\n        \"devcontainer\",\n        help=\"Resolve the architecture-scoped devcontainer resource names \"\n        \"(#677): container, home volume and SSH port all carry the arch, so \"\n        \"amd64 and arm64 can be up at once without silently sharing a home\",\n    )\n    devcontainer_sub = devcontainer_parser.add_subparsers(\n        dest=\"devcontainer_command\", help=\"Devcontainer name commands\"\n    )\n    devcontainer_sub.add_parser(\n        \"env\",\n        help=\"Print shell exports for every localEnv substitution \"\n        'devcontainer.json makes — consumed as `eval \"$(… devcontainer env)\"`',\n    )\n    devcontainer_name_parser = devcontainer_sub.add_parser(\n        \"name\", help=\"Print one resolved devcontainer resource name\"\n    )\n    devcontainer_name_parser.add_argument(\n        \"field\", choices=NAME_FIELDS, help=\"Which name to print\"\n    )\n    migrate_parser = devcontainer_sub.add_parser(\n        \"migrate-home\",\n        help=\"Copy a pre-#677 home volume into this architecture's volume. \"\n        \"Dry-run by default; the source is never deleted\",\n    )\n    migrate_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--apply\",\n        action=\"store_true\",\n        help=\"Actually run the copy. Without it nothing is mutated — a bulk \"\n        \"operation whose bare invocation writes cannot be used to look\",\n    )\n    migrate_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--platform\",\n        default=None,\n        help=\"The platform whose architecture built the home being copied. \"\n        \"Required unless $DOTFILES_PLATFORM pins one: the pre-#677 volume name \"\n        \"records no architecture, so an unpinned run would take it from this \"\n        \"HOST and could name the target for the wrong machine\",\n    )\n    subparsers.add_parser(\n        \"classifier-axes\",\n        help=\"Enforce classifier axis enumeration: every registered \"\n        \"classifier's declared axes must equal the set DERIVED from its own \"\n        \"code (its params + every subject field its predicates read). #601 \"\n        \"lost four review rounds to an axis a sibling predicate already \"\n        \"consumed but the author's enumeration omitted\",\n    )\n    subparsers.add_parser(\n        \"lock-check\",\n        help=\"Reject a lockfile that lost platform coverage vs HEAD. A bare \"\n        \"`mise lock` or any `mise install` on macOS re-locks the whole file for \"\n        \"this platform and drops the linux conda entries the amd64 image needs \"\n        \"(#370, jdx/mise#7700) — measured: linux-x64 628 -> 80\",\n    )","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"4ad1112f-7038-46b9-b07e-657701e36748","timestamp":"2026-08-10T11:34:43.573Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e1a31753-489f-45ab-9447-23255f6dde36","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"eeaf6a41-3473-4c9e-a681-cca17051c6ab","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"feat/677-arch-scoped-devcontainer-names"}

{"parentUuid":"d4e1b760-473d-4edc-b91f-e27bf6c05116","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"568a3094-ff17-4144-8041-81815078475a","agentId":"ae619a3115cc0fa20","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01EEmR7pRTBqkukkcA4T2ubo","type":"tool_result","content":"# DOCKER_DEFAULT_PLATFORM is task-scoped so it only affects `devcontainer up`\n# (and its child docker calls) — host-level docker/colima invocations are\n# unaffected. Its VALUE now comes from the global `[env] DOTFILES_PLATFORM`\n# (#673), which is the one place the platform is chosen; the scoping is what\n# stays task-local, because DOTFILES_PLATFORM is inert to docker while\n# DOCKER_DEFAULT_PLATFORM is not.\n#\n# DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT is the HOST-side port for the inbound shell\n# (Requirement R1: `ssh ${USER}@localhost -p 4444` opens a shell in the\n# container). Container-internal sshd listens on 2222 (feature hardcoded);\n# devcontainer.json maps ${DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT}:2222 via appPort.\n# Override per-clone via mise.local.toml on port collision without losing\n# named volumes — volume names do NOT include the port (C10/C11/C12).\n#\n# Outbound agent forwarding (Requirement R2: `ssh -T git@github.com` from\n# inside container) is independent; uses the host-TCP + container-unix-\n# socket proxy pattern spawned from initializeCommand/postStartCommand.\n# Research: docs/research/runs/research-20260407-ssh-devcontainer/report.md.\n#\n# Image pull: handled inside `devcontainer up` via buildx (the\n# Dockerfile.host-user `FROM ${BASE_IMAGE}` triggers a registry pull\n[tasks.verify]\ndescription = \"Run the structured verification contracts (suites.toml) — the third local gate\"\n# Canonical entrypoint. AGENTS.md previously told agents to run a bare\n# `dotfiles-setup verify run`, which is NOT on PATH and returns rc=127 (hit\n# 2026-07-20 while running the pre-commit gates). The console script only\n# exists inside the uv-managed venv, so it must be invoked through `uv run\n# --project python`. Wrapping it as a task is what `mise-tasks-only.md`\n# requires of every recurring workflow, and it keeps the AGENTS.md quick-start\n# shorter than the broken form it replaces.\nrun = 'uv run --project python dotfiles-setup verify run'\n\n[tasks.verify-image]\ndescription = \"Spot-check cookbook paths in :dev (cargo/rustup cookbook layout + core toolchains)\"\n# Source of truth for what's INSIDE :dev is .devcontainer/mise-system.toml,\n# NOT root mise.toml. Root mise.toml tools (hk, pkl, the 5 markdown\n# linters, etc.) live on the HOST, not in the image. Keep assertions here\n# scoped to what mise-system.toml actually provides.\nenv = { BASE_IMAGE = \"{{ env.BASE_IMAGE | default(value='ghcr.io/ray-manaloto/dotfiles-devcontainer:dev') }}\", DOCKER_DEFAULT_PLATFORM = \"{{ env.DOTFILES_PLATFORM }}\" }\nrun = '''\n# Review finding [29]: --pull=never — an absent local tag must fail loud\n# (use `mise run sync` to fetch via buildkit), never classic-pull ~38GB.\ndocker run --rm --pull=never \"$BASE_IMAGE\" bash -lc '\n  set -euo pipefail\n  # Cookbook cargo/rustup homes (PR-2 commit F)\n  ls /usr/local/share/cargo/bin\n  ls /usr/local/share/rustup/toolchains\n  test ! -d /opt/cargo\n  test ! -d /opt/rustup\n  # Core toolchain reachability via mise activation\n  cargo --version\n  rustc --version\n  clang --version\n'\n'''\n\n[tasks.smoke]\ndescription = \"Run tier 1-3 smoke checks inside the running devcontainer\"\nrun = \"devcontainer exec --workspace-folder . scripts/devcontainer-smoke.sh\"\n\n[tasks.sh]\ndescription = \"Open an interactive shell in the running devcontainer\"\nalias = \"shell\"\nrun = \"devcontainer exec --workspace-folder . bash -l\"\n\n[tasks.ps]\ndescription = \"Show the running devcontainer + its named volumes\"\nrun = '''\nset -euo pipefail\n_stem=\"$(uv run --project python dotfiles-setup devcontainer name legacy-volume)\"\n_stem=\"${_stem%-home}\"\n# Both architectures, if both are up — the label is per-FOLDER, not per-arch,\n# so this is the view that shows a collision (#677).\ndocker ps --filter \"label=devcontainer.local_folder=$PWD\" --format 'table {{\"{{\"}}.Names{{\"}}\"}}\\t{{\"{{\"}}.Status{{\"}}\"}}\\t{{\"{{\"}}.Ports{{\"}}\"}}'\n# Scoped to this clone's hashed volumes: every arch's home plus any surviving\n# pre-#677 one, which shares the stem and so shows up here too.\ndocker volume ls --format '{{\"{{\"}}.Name{{\"}}\"}}' | grep -E \"^${_stem}\" || true\n'''\n\n[tasks.dev]\ndescription = \"Full devloop: up → smoke (sequential)\"\n# NOTE: `depends = [...]` in mise runs tasks in parallel when independent\n# — not what we want here. A sequential shell body forces ordering while\n# keeping each sub-task independently runnable.\n#\n# Image pulling is delegated to devcontainer-cli (called by `mise run up`).\n# Standalone `docker pull` was removed — it bypassed the lifecycle and\n# masked silent failures (PR #86 review). Use `mise run dev-rebuild`\n# for an explicit force-refresh.\nrun = '''\nset -euo pipefail\nmise run up\nmise run smoke\n'''\n\n[tasks.persistence]\ndescription = \"Verify .devcontainer/mise-system.toml tools survive a devcontainer stop/up cycle\"\n# Source of truth for the expected tool set is .devcontainer/mise-system.toml\n# (installed to /usr/local/share/mise/config.toml inside :dev). This task\n# takes the runtime `mise ls --json --installed` view as ground truth,\n# cross-checked against a minimum count parsed from mise-system.toml, and\n# asserts every (tool, version) entry survives a stop/up cycle unchanged.\n#\n# Requires a running devcontainer on entry; the devloop\n# `mise run dev` / `mise run verify-local` brings it up first.\nrun = '''\nset -euo pipefail\nSNAPSHOT=/tmp/mise-persistence-before.json\nAFTER=/tmp/mise-persistence-after.json\nSYSTEM_TOML=.devcontainer/mise-system.toml\n\n# (0) Derive the expected minimum tool count from mise-system.toml.\n#     Python tomllib is the canonical parser; python 3.14 is pinned in\n#     project mise.toml so this is always available during local runs.\nEXPECTED_MIN=$(python3 -c \"\nimport tomllib, sys\nwith open('$SYSTEM_TOML', 'rb') as f:\n    cfg = tomllib.load(f)\nprint(len(cfg.get('tools', {})))\n\")\necho \"==> mise-system.toml declares $EXPECTED_MIN tools\"\n\n# (1) Pre-teardown: snapshot resolved versions.\n#     `mise doctor` is advisory only — inside the container it sees the\n#     workspace /workspaces/dotfiles/mise.toml which legitimately lists\n#     host-only tools (colima, lima, @devcontainers/cli, agnix, markdown\n#     linters) that are NOT in mise-system.toml. Doctor reports them as\n#     \"(missing)\" with a non-zero exit. The TRUE persistence invariant is\n#     the snapshot diff (step 4) being empty; doctor is logged but never\n#     fails the gate. Architectural cleanup (untrust the workspace config\n#     in-container) is tracked as a follow-up — see issue #70 thread.\necho \"==> mise doctor (pre-teardown, advisory)\"\ndevcontainer exec --workspace-folder . bash -lc 'mise doctor || true'\n\necho \"==> capturing pre-teardown snapshot\"\ndevcontainer exec --workspace-folder . bash -lc 'mise ls --json --installed' > \"$SNAPSHOT\"\nPRE_COUNT=$(jq 'length' \"$SNAPSHOT\")\necho \"pre-teardown installed tool count: $PRE_COUNT\"\nif [ \"$PRE_COUNT\" -lt \"$EXPECTED_MIN\" ]; then\n  echo \"FAIL: fewer tools installed ($PRE_COUNT) than mise-system.toml declares ($EXPECTED_MIN)\" >&2\n  exit 1\nfi\n\n# (1a) v6 single-home-volume contract: write an in-volume canary file.\n#      This file lives on the persistent home volume; if the volume is\n#      correctly mounted across stop/up, it must survive. Contrast with\n#      /tmp (ephemeral per container) — the canary proves the VOLUME is\n#      the thing persisting, not just the container state.\necho \"==> writing in-volume persistence canary\"\nCANARY_CONTENT=\"canary-$(date +%s)-$$\"\ndevcontainer exec --workspace-folder . bash -lc \"printf '%s' '${CANARY_CONTENT}' > ~/.local/tmp/persistence-canary.txt\"\n\n# (2) Stop/up cycle — the actual persistence test.\necho \"==> tearing container down\"\nmise run stop\necho \"==> bringing container back up\"","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"7e9b58eb-5a06-429b-b40e-221ff2443d7c","timestamp":"2026-08-10T11:35:02.243Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d4e1b760-473d-4edc-b91f-e27bf6c05116","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"eeaf6a41-3473-4c9e-a681-cca17051c6ab","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"feat/677-arch-scoped-devcontainer-names"}

{"parentUuid":"ee710f2f-fe55-4ef9-b3f9-ce28cf8ecac8","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"568a3094-ff17-4144-8041-81815078475a","agentId":"ae619a3115cc0fa20","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019uW5yastoRYAmijDf91pr9","type":"tool_result","content":"940\tmise run verify-ssh-inbound\n941\tmise run verify-secrets\n942\t'''\n943\t\n944\t[tasks.prune]\n945\tdescription = \"Full devcontainer prune: stop container, remove volumes, images, and build cache\"\n946\trun = '''\n947\tset -euo pipefail\n948\t# Scoped prune prevents deleting sibling-clone hashed home volumes\n949\t# (Codex v3 finding — broad-prefix prune was cross-clone destructive).\n950\t# The stem is `dotfiles-<basename>-<user>-<hash>`, shared by every\n951\t# architecture's volume AND by the pre-#677 one, so prune stays whole-clone\n952\t# while remaining blind to other clones.\n953\t_stem=\"$(uv run --project python dotfiles-setup devcontainer name legacy-volume)\"\n954\t_stem=\"${_stem%-home}\"\n955\t_hash=\"$(uv run --project python dotfiles-setup devcontainer name hash)\"\n956\t\n957\t# Differentiate \"no match\" (acceptable, idempotent) from \"docker command\n958\t# failed\" (must be loud) per feedback_never_suppress_warnings. Pattern:\n959\t# capture command output, check non-empty, then act. Daemon failures\n960\t# propagate via set -e on the capturing subshell.\n961\techo \"==> stopping container\"\n962\tcontainer_ids=\"$(docker ps -aq --filter \"label=devcontainer.local_folder=$PWD\")\"\n963\tif [ -n \"${container_ids}\" ]; then\n964\t  # shellcheck disable=SC2086\n965\t  docker rm -f ${container_ids}\n966\telse\n967\t  echo \"    (no container labeled for $PWD — already stopped)\"\n968\tfi\n969\t\n970\techo \"==> removing named volumes (scoped to hash ${_hash})\"\n971\tall_volumes=\"$(docker volume ls --format '{{\"{{\"}}.Name{{\"}}\"}}')\"\n972\thashed_volumes=\"$(echo \"${all_volumes}\" | grep -E \"^${_stem}\" || true)\"\n973\tif [ -n \"${hashed_volumes}\" ]; then\n974\t  echo \"${hashed_volumes}\" | xargs docker volume rm\n975\telse\n976\t  echo \"    (no hashed volumes for ${_hash} — already removed)\"\n977\tfi\n978\t\n979\t# Also clean up any ORPHANED legacy volumes from the pre-v6 per-directory\n980\t# layout (mise-user / cargo-user / rustup-user). Bounded by the\n981\t# basename+user prefix so only this repo's orphans are targeted.\n982\tlegacy_volumes=\"$(echo \"${all_volumes}\" | grep -E \"^dotfiles-$(basename \"$PWD\")-${USER}-(mise-user|cargo-user|rustup-user)$\" || true)\"\n983\tif [ -n \"${legacy_volumes}\" ]; then\n984\t  echo \"==> removing legacy v5 volumes\"\n985\t  echo \"${legacy_volumes}\" | xargs docker volume rm\n986\tfi\n987\t\n988\techo \"==> removing overlay images\"\n989\tall_images=\"$(docker images --format '{{\"{{\"}}.Repository{{\"}}\"}}:{{\"{{\"}}.Tag{{\"}}\"}}')\"\n990\toverlay_images=\"$(echo \"${all_images}\" | grep vsc-dotfiles || true)\"\n991\tif [ -n \"${overlay_images}\" ]; then\n992\t  echo \"${overlay_images}\" | xargs docker rmi\n993\telse\n994\t  echo \"    (no vsc-dotfiles overlay images — already removed)\"\n995\tfi\n996\t\n997\techo \"==> pruning build cache\"\n998\tdocker builder prune -f\n999\techo \"==> done\"\n1000\t'''\n1001\t\n1002\t[tasks.stop]\n1003\tdescription = \"Tear the devcontainer down (no 'devcontainer down' verb in v0.85.0)\"\n1004\talias = \"down\"\n1005\t# devcontainer CLI v0.85.0 has no 'down' verb (verified 2026-04-06 via\n1006\t# `devcontainer --help`; verbs: up, set-up, build, run-user-commands,\n1007\t# read-configuration, outdated, upgrade, features, templates, exec).\n1008\t# Teardown uses `docker rm -f` filtered on the workspace-folder label\n1009\t# that the devcontainer CLI applies to every container it creates.\n1010\t#\n1011\t# R2 outbound now uses Docker Desktop's native magic socket — no host\n1012\t# proxy to tear down (deleted in #77 stage 2).\n1013\trun = '''\n1014\tset -euo pipefail\n1015\tcontainer_ids=\"$(docker ps -aq --filter \"label=devcontainer.local_folder=$PWD\")\"\n1016\tif [ -n \"${container_ids}\" ]; then\n1017\t  # shellcheck disable=SC2086\n1018\t  docker rm -f ${container_ids}\n1019\telse\n1020\t  echo \"(no container labeled for $PWD — already stopped)\"\n1021\tfi\n1022\t'''\n1023\t\n1024\t[tasks.build]"}]},"uuid":"61810c7a-91c3-4552-8c3e-bd309cda696c","timestamp":"2026-08-10T11:35:09.160Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ee710f2f-fe55-4ef9-b3f9-ce28cf8ecac8","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"eeaf6a41-3473-4c9e-a681-cca17051c6ab","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"feat/677-arch-scoped-devcontainer-names"}

{"parentUuid":"54f29847-a15e-42ef-a46a-7ef5cc60c673","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"568a3094-ff17-4144-8041-81815078475a","agentId":"ae619a3115cc0fa20","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JWqowMF21ZKtkfscJ6KBXY","type":"tool_result","content":"818\t[tasks.verify-arch]\n819\tdescription = \"R3: container runs as the targeted platform (not host passthrough)\"\n820\t# Proves Requirement 3: the container is the architecture we ASKED for,\n821\t# regardless of host arch. Three independent signals: (1) docker image\n822\t# manifest architecture, (2) container uname -m, (3) container arch. All\n823\t# three must agree with $DOTFILES_PLATFORM. The host can be arm64 (macOS ARM\n824\t# via Docker Desktop Rosetta, or the deferred Colima VZ+Rosetta path) or\n825\t# amd64 (Linux CI runner); we don't check the host.\n826\t#\n827\t# #673: the expected values are DERIVED from the one platform parameter\n828\t# rather than hard-coded to amd64/x86_64 — the triple→arch and triple→uname\n829\t# mappings live in python/src/dotfiles_setup/platform_target.py\n830\t# (zero-bash-logic); the two lines below are assignments, not logic.\n831\trun = '''\n832\tset -euo pipefail\n833\texpected_arch=\"$(uv run --project python dotfiles-setup platform arch)\"\n834\texpected_machine=\"$(uv run --project python dotfiles-setup platform machine)\"\n835\t# #677: select by NAME, not by the devcontainer.local_folder label. That label\n836\t# is per-FOLDER, so once both architectures can be up in one directory it\n837\t# matches TWO containers and the old `| head -1` picked an arbitrary one —\n838\t# R3 would then have asserted the architecture of whichever docker listed\n839\t# first, which is a false pass in exactly the direction R3 exists to catch.\n840\tcontainer_name=\"$(uv run --project python dotfiles-setup devcontainer name container)\"\n841\tif [ -z \"$(docker ps -q --filter \"name=^${container_name}$\")\" ]; then\n842\t  echo \"FAIL: no running devcontainer named ${container_name}\" >&2\n843\t  exit 1\n844\tfi\n845\techo \"==> docker image architecture\"\n846\timage_id=$(docker inspect \"${container_name}\" --format '{{\"{{\"}}.Image{{\"}}\"}}')\n847\timage_arch=$(docker image inspect \"${image_id}\" --format '{{\"{{\"}}.Architecture{{\"}}\"}}')\n848\techo \"image: ${image_arch}\"\n849\t[ \"${image_arch}\" = \"${expected_arch}\" ] || { echo \"FAIL: image arch is ${image_arch}, expected ${expected_arch}\" >&2; exit 1; }\n850\t\n851\techo \"==> container uname -m\"\n852\tuname_m=$(docker exec \"${container_name}\" uname -m)\n853\techo \"uname -m: ${uname_m}\"\n854\t[ \"${uname_m}\" = \"${expected_machine}\" ] || { echo \"FAIL: uname -m is ${uname_m}, expected ${expected_machine}\" >&2; exit 1; }\n855\t\n856\techo \"==> container arch\"\n857\tarch_out=$(docker exec \"${container_name}\" arch)\n858\techo \"arch: ${arch_out}\"\n859\t[ \"${arch_out}\" = \"${expected_machine}\" ] || { echo \"FAIL: arch is ${arch_out}, expected ${expected_machine}\" >&2; exit 1; }\n860\t\n861\techo \"OK: R3 container is ${DOTFILES_PLATFORM} ${expected_machine} on all three signals\"\n862\t'''\n863\t\n864\t[tasks.verify-ssh-inbound]\n865\tdescription = \"R1: ssh ${USER}@localhost -p ${DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT} succeeds from the host\"\n866\t# Proves Requirement 1: the ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/sshd feature\n867\t# is running sshd inside the container, the host port mapping is live,\n868\t# and the authorized_keys copy in postCreateCommand worked (host user\n869\t# can log in without a password). Runs on the HOST, not inside the\n870\t# container. StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new + UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/\n871\t# null so we don't pollute the host's known_hosts with the container's\n872\t# ephemeral host keys.\n873\tenv = { DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT = \"{{ env.DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT | default(value='') }}\" }\n874\trun = '''\n875\tset -euo pipefail\n876\t# Resolved, not defaulted (#677): the port is derived from workspace+arch\n877\t# unless DEVCONTAINER_SSH_PORT pins one, so R1 must ask the same resolver\n878\t# `mise run up` asked rather than restating a number.\n879\tport=\"$(uv run --project python dotfiles-setup devcontainer name port)\"\n880\techo \"==> probing ssh ${USER}@localhost -p ${port}\"\n881\tout=$(ssh -o BatchMode=yes \\\n882\t          -o StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new \\\n883\t          -o UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null \\\n884\t          -o LogLevel=ERROR \\\n885\t          -o ConnectTimeout=10 \\\n886\t          -p \"${port}\" \"${USER}@localhost\" 'hostname && whoami')\n887\techo \"${out}\"\n888\techo \"${out}\" | grep -q \"${USER}\" || {\n889\t  echo \"FAIL: whoami did not return ${USER}\" >&2\n890\t  exit 1\n891\t}\n892\techo \"OK: R1 inbound ssh ${USER}@localhost -p ${port} works\"\n893\t'''\n894\t\n895\t[tasks.verify-secrets]\n896\tdescription = \"S1: doppler env file exists and contains secrets\"\n897\tenv = { DOPPLER_PROJECT = \"dotfiles\", DOPPLER_CONFIG = \"{{ env.DOPPLER_CONFIG | default(value='dev_personal') }}\" }"}]},"uuid":"b2b87f4f-f762-4514-97f6-36b64eebe69d","timestamp":"2026-08-10T11:35:20.912Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"54f29847-a15e-42ef-a46a-7ef5cc60c673","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"eeaf6a41-3473-4c9e-a681-cca17051c6ab","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"feat/677-arch-scoped-devcontainer-names"}

{"parentUuid":"258c3190-db62-419d-84e9-9d14a983d98d","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"ae619a3115cc0fa20","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Cdtzr1NCjY9Sdbjff3LXb","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01MqYM6LaZ35DCLYReoF3GZ2","name":"Read","input":{"file_path":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles/python/src/dotfiles_setup/platform_target.py"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2737,"cache_read_input_tokens":124874,"output_tokens":691,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2737},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":691,"cache_read_input_tokens":124874,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2737,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2737,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdtzqxFvX6Lg5osbcbFYz","attributionSkill":"code-review","type":"assistant","uuid":"8b450a21-ebe8-49b5-a923-460893173f8c","timestamp":"2026-08-10T11:35:31.429Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"eeaf6a41-3473-4c9e-a681-cca17051c6ab","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"feat/677-arch-scoped-devcontainer-names"}
{"parentUuid":"8b450a21-ebe8-49b5-a923-460893173f8c","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"568a3094-ff17-4144-8041-81815078475a","agentId":"ae619a3115cc0fa20","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01MqYM6LaZ35DCLYReoF3GZ2","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Copyright (c) 2026 Raymond Manaloto\n2\t\"\"\"The one platform parameter (#673).\n3\t\n4\tThe target platform used to be a literal — ``linux/amd64/v2`` — repeated across\n5\t``docker-bake.hcl``, three ``mise.toml`` task envs, ``devcontainer.json``, five\n6\tPython modules, ``scripts/benchmark-docker.sh`` and two verification contracts.\n7\tChanging the target meant editing every one of them, and **missing two produces\n8\ta container that runs one architecture while a probe asserts another** — not a\n9\tcrash, a false pass.\n10\t\n11\tThis module is the single resolver every one of those sites now goes through.\n12\t\n13\tResolution order\n14\t----------------\n15\t\n16\t1. an explicit ``override`` argument (the caller asked for a specific arch),\n17\t2. the ``DOTFILES_PLATFORM`` environment variable — **the repo's pin**,\n18\t3. the host's own native triple.\n19\t\n20\tStep 3 is why the parameter \"defaults to the host architecture\" (#673 AC1) while\n21\tbehaviour stays unchanged (#673 AC4): the repo pins step 2 to ``linux/amd64/v2``\n22\tin ``mise.toml``'s global ``[env]``, so every flow that runs under mise resolves\n23\tto exactly what it resolved to before. When #676 publishes both architectures\n24\tand #678 brings up a native arm64 container, deleting that pin is what flips the\n25\tdefault to native — no other site changes.\n26\t\n27\tA platform is a **triple**, not an arch word\n28\t--------------------------------------------\n29\t\n30\t``linux/amd64/v2`` carries a *microarchitecture level*; arm64's analogue is\n31\t``linux/arm64/v8``. Every function here takes and returns the full triple, and\n32\t:func:`os_arch` is the explicit way to drop the level when a consumer (docker's\n33\t``--platform`` for an apt probe) only cares about the architecture.\n34\t\n35\tWhere the literal is still allowed\n36\t----------------------------------\n37\t\n38\tTwo files, listed in :data:`DEFAULT_LITERAL_SITES`. ``mise.toml`` is the pin\n39\tevery mise-run flow reads. ``docker-bake.hcl`` must repeat it because CI's\n40\t``docker/bake-action`` jobs do not run under mise and HCL cannot read a TOML\n41\tfile — see :func:`find_violations`, which forbids the literal everywhere else,\n42\tand :func:`find_default_drift`, which fails if the two ever disagree.\n43\tCollapsing them into one generated value is #680's codegen job, not this\n44\tticket's.\n45\t\"\"\"\n46\t\n47\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n48\t\n49\timport logging\n50\timport os\n51\timport re\n52\timport subprocess\n53\timport sys\n54\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n55\tfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING\n56\t\n57\tif TYPE_CHECKING:\n58\t    from pathlib import Path\n59\t\n60\tlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n61\t\n62\t__all__ = [\n63\t    \"DEFAULT_LITERAL_SITES\",\n64\t    \"PLATFORM_ENV_VAR\",\n65\t    \"PLATFORM_FIELDS\",\n66\t    \"PlatformLiteral\",\n67\t    \"expected_uname_machine\",\n68\t    \"find_default_drift\",\n69\t    \"find_violations\",\n70\t    \"host_arch\",\n71\t    \"host_platform\",\n72\t    \"is_emulated\",\n73\t    \"normalize_arch\",\n74\t    \"os_arch\",\n75\t    \"platform_arch\",\n76\t    \"platform_field\",\n77\t    \"platform_literals_main\",\n78\t    \"platform_main\",\n79\t    \"resolve_platform\",\n80\t]\n81\t\n82\tPLATFORM_ENV_VAR = \"DOTFILES_PLATFORM\"\n83\t\n84\t# Docker's architecture names, keyed by every spelling a host or a platform\n85\t# string may use for them. `uname -m` says x86_64/aarch64; docker says\n86\t# amd64/arm64; the two are not interchangeable and conflating them is how a\n87\t# probe ends up asserting the wrong machine.\n88\t_ARCH_ALIASES = {\n89\t    \"x86_64\": \"amd64\",\n90\t    \"amd64\": \"amd64\",\n91\t    \"arm64\": \"arm64\",\n92\t    \"aarch64\": \"arm64\",\n93\t}\n94\t\n95\t# The microarchitecture level this project targets per architecture. amd64/v2 is\n96\t# the baseline the image is built for; arm64/v8 is its analogue.\n97\t_MICROARCH_LEVEL = {\"amd64\": \"v2\", \"arm64\": \"v8\"}\n98\t\n99\t# What `uname -m` reports inside a container of each architecture — the value\n100\t# `mise run verify-arch` (R3) compares against.\n101\t_UNAME_MACHINE = {\"amd64\": \"x86_64\", \"arm64\": \"aarch64\"}\n102\t\n103\t# The two files permitted to carry the literal; see the module docstring.\n104\tDEFAULT_LITERAL_SITES = (\"mise.toml\", \"docker-bake.hcl\")\n105\t\n106\t# `linux/<arch>` with an optional microarchitecture level. Deliberately matches\n107\t# the arch words too (`linux/amd64` without a level), because a site that drops\n108\t# the level is exactly the split-brain this gate exists to catch.\n109\t_LITERAL_RE = re.compile(r\"linux/(?:amd64|arm64|x86_64|aarch64)(?:/v\\d+)?\")\n110\t\n111\t# Files scanned for a re-appearing literal. Prose and archived evidence are out\n112\t# of scope — a research report records what was true when it was written, and\n113\t# rewriting it would falsify the record (`agent-artifact-conventions.md`).\n114\t_SCANNED_SUFFIXES = (\".py\", \".toml\", \".hcl\", \".json\", \".sh\", \".yml\", \".yaml\")\n115\t\n116\t# Tests legitimately name concrete platforms: a fixture asserting that another\n117\t# architecture bumps a content hash cannot express itself through the resolver\n118\t# it is testing without becoming tautological (`tests/AGENTS.md`).\n119\t_SCAN_EXCLUDED_PREFIXES = (\n120\t    \"docs/\",\n121\t    \"missions/\",\n122\t    \"plugins/\",\n123\t    \"tests/\",\n124\t)\n125\t\n126\t# This module is the definition site: it composes every triple from\n127\t# `_MICROARCH_LEVEL` and never hard-codes one, but it must be able to NAME them\n128\t# — in its docstring, in the scan pattern, and in the explanation of why a\n129\t# triple is not an arch word. Documentation that cannot use the example is\n130\t# worse documentation, and this is the one file a reader comes to for the\n131\t# answer. Nothing here issues a `--platform`, so the exemption costs no\n132\t# coverage.\n133\t_SCAN_EXCLUDED_PATHS = (\"python/src/dotfiles_setup/platform_target.py\",)\n134\t\n135\t\n136\tdef normalize_arch(token: str) -> str | None:\n137\t    \"\"\"Docker's architecture name for ``token``, or ``None`` if unrecognised.\"\"\"\n138\t    return _ARCH_ALIASES.get(token.strip().lower())\n139\t\n140\t\n141\tdef host_arch() -> str:\n142\t    \"\"\"This host's docker architecture name (``amd64`` / ``arm64``).\n143\t\n144\t    Raises ``ValueError`` on an architecture this project has no\n145\t    microarchitecture level for, rather than guessing one — a wrong level\n146\t    silently builds an image the host cannot run.\n147\t    \"\"\"\n148\t    machine = os.uname().machine\n149\t    arch = normalize_arch(machine)\n150\t    if arch is None:\n151\t        msg = (\n152\t            f\"unsupported host architecture {machine!r}: this project targets \"\n153\t            f\"{sorted(_MICROARCH_LEVEL)} only\"\n154\t        )\n155\t        raise ValueError(msg)\n156\t    return arch\n157\t\n158\t\n159\tdef host_platform() -> str:\n160\t    \"\"\"This host's native triple (``linux/arm64/v8`` on an M-series Mac).\"\"\"\n161\t    arch = host_arch()\n162\t    return f\"linux/{arch}/{_MICROARCH_LEVEL[arch]}\"\n163\t\n164\t\n165\tdef resolve_platform(\n166\t    override: str | None = None,\n167\t    *,\n168\t    env: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n169\t) -> str:\n170\t    \"\"\"The platform triple to target: ``override`` → repo pin → host native.\n171\t\n172\t    ``env`` defaults to the ambient process environment and exists so callers\n173\t    (and tests) can resolve against an explicit mapping.\n174\t    \"\"\"\n175\t    if override:\n176\t        return override\n177\t    environ = os.environ if env is None else env\n178\t    pinned = environ.get(PLATFORM_ENV_VAR, \"\").strip()\n179\t    if pinned:\n180\t        return pinned\n181\t    return host_platform()\n182\t\n183\t\n184\tdef platform_arch(platform: str) -> str:\n185\t    \"\"\"The docker architecture name inside a platform triple.\n186\t\n187\t    Raises ``ValueError`` rather than defaulting, because every caller uses the\n188\t    answer to assert something — an assertion built on a guess cannot fail\n189\t    honestly.\n190\t    \"\"\"\n191\t    for part in platform.split(\"/\"):\n192\t        arch = normalize_arch(part)\n193\t        if arch is not None:\n194\t            return arch\n195\t    msg = f\"no recognised architecture in platform {platform!r}\"\n196\t    raise ValueError(msg)\n197\t\n198\t\n199\tdef os_arch(platform: str) -> str:\n200\t    \"\"\"``platform`` with its microarchitecture level dropped (``linux/amd64``).\n201\t\n202\t    Used where a consumer resolves per-architecture artifacts and the level is\n203\t    meaningless — apt package pins are published per architecture, not per\n204\t    microarchitecture level.\n205\t    \"\"\"\n206\t    return f\"linux/{platform_arch(platform)}\"\n207\t\n208\t\n209\tdef expected_uname_machine(platform: str) -> str:\n210\t    \"\"\"What ``uname -m`` reports inside a container of ``platform`` (R3).\"\"\"\n211\t    return _UNAME_MACHINE[platform_arch(platform)]\n212\t\n213\t\n214\tdef is_emulated(platform: str) -> bool:\n215\t    \"\"\"True when this host's CPU cannot *natively* execute ``platform``.\n216\t\n217\t    ThreadSanitizer's shadow-memory layout requires a native x86_64 process;\n218\t    under Rosetta/QEMU emulation (arm64 host running an amd64 image) the TSan\n219\t    RUN aborts with an ASLR/\"unexpected memory mapping\" fatal. The host↔target\n220\t    mismatch is detected here — Python knows the host arch via ``os.uname()`` —\n221\t    rather than by probing ``binfmt_misc`` in-container, because the emulator\n222\t    marker is not reliably visible inside the container.\n223\t    \"\"\"\n224\t    try:\n225\t        target = platform_arch(platform)\n226\t    except ValueError:\n227\t        return False\n228\t    try:\n229\t        return target != host_arch()\n230\t    except ValueError:\n231\t        return False\n232\t\n233\t\n234\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n235\tclass PlatformLiteral:\n236\t    \"\"\"One re-appearing platform literal found by :func:`find_violations`.\"\"\"\n237\t\n238\t    path: str\n239\t    line: int\n240\t    literal: str\n241\t\n242\t    def render(self) -> str:\n243\t        \"\"\"One reviewer-facing line naming the site and the fix.\"\"\"\n244\t        return (\n245\t            f\"{self.path}:{self.line}: hard-coded platform literal \"\n246\t            f\"{self.literal!r} — resolve it from {PLATFORM_ENV_VAR} \"\n247\t            f\"(config) or dotfiles_setup.platform_target.resolve_platform() \"\n248\t            f\"(Python) instead\"\n249\t        )\n250\t\n251\t\n252\tdef _tracked_files(repo_root: Path) -> list[str]:\n253\t    proc = subprocess.run(\n254\t        [\"git\", \"-C\", str(repo_root), \"ls-files\", \"-z\"],\n255\t        capture_output=True,\n256\t        text=True,\n257\t        check=True,\n258\t    )\n259\t    return [entry for entry in proc.stdout.split(\"\\0\") if entry]\n260\t\n261\t\n262\tdef _in_scope(rel_path: str) -> bool:\n263\t    if not rel_path.endswith(_SCANNED_SUFFIXES):\n264\t        return False\n265\t    if rel_path.startswith(_SCAN_EXCLUDED_PREFIXES):\n266\t        return False\n267\t    if rel_path in _SCAN_EXCLUDED_PATHS:\n268\t        return False\n269\t    return rel_path not in DEFAULT_LITERAL_SITES\n270\t\n271\t\n272\tdef find_violations(repo_root: Path) -> list[PlatformLiteral]:\n273\t    \"\"\"Every platform literal outside the two permitted default sites.\n274\t\n275\t    Machine-enforced completeness (#673 AC3): careful editing threads the\n276\t    parameter through the sites you remembered, and this finds the ones you did\n277\t    not. It scans the *tracked* tree so a literal cannot arrive via a file the\n278\t    author forgot to add.\n279\t    \"\"\"\n280\t    violations: list[PlatformLiteral] = []\n281\t    for rel_path in _tracked_files(repo_root):\n282\t        if not _in_scope(rel_path):\n283\t            continue\n284\t        target = repo_root / rel_path\n285\t        try:\n286\t            text = target.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n287\t        except OSError, UnicodeDecodeError:\n288\t            continue\n289\t        violations.extend(\n290\t            PlatformLiteral(path=rel_path, line=lineno, literal=match.group(0))\n291\t            for lineno, line in enumerate(text.splitlines(), start=1)\n292\t            for match in _LITERAL_RE.finditer(line)\n293\t        )\n294\t    return violations\n295\t\n296\t\n297\t# The declaration each permitted site must carry the default in. mise.toml's\n298\t# is templated so a per-clone mise.local.toml override still works; bake's is a\n299\t# plain HCL variable default.\n300\t_MISE_DEFAULT_RE = re.compile(\n301\t    rf\"^{PLATFORM_ENV_VAR}\\s*=.*default\\(value='([^']+)'\\)\", re.MULTILINE\n302\t)\n303\t_BAKE_DEFAULT_RE = re.compile(\n304\t    r'variable\\s+\"PLATFORM\"\\s*\\{[^}]*default\\s*=\\s*\"([^\"]+)\"', re.DOTALL\n305\t)\n306\t\n307\t\n308\tdef _read_default(\n309\t    repo_root: Path, rel_path: str, pattern: re.Pattern[str]\n310\t) -> str | None:\n311\t    try:\n312\t        text = (repo_root / rel_path).read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n313\t    except OSError:\n314\t        return None\n315\t    match = pattern.search(text)\n316\t    return match.group(1) if match else None\n317\t\n318\t\n319\tdef find_default_drift(repo_root: Path) -> str | None:\n320\t    \"\"\"A message when the two permitted defaults disagree, else ``None``.\n321\t\n322\t    ``docker-bake.hcl`` repeats the literal because CI's bake jobs do not run\n323\t    under mise and HCL cannot read a TOML file. That duplication is only safe\n324\t    while the two are equal, so it is checked rather than trusted — a retarget\n325\t    that edits one and forgets the other would publish an image of one\n326\t    architecture for a devcontainer that asks for the other.\n327\t    \"\"\"\n328\t    mise_default = _read_default(repo_root, \"mise.toml\", _MISE_DEFAULT_RE)\n329\t    bake_default = _read_default(repo_root, \"docker-bake.hcl\", _BAKE_DEFAULT_RE)\n330\t    if mise_default is None:\n331\t        return (\n332\t            f\"mise.toml no longer declares a {PLATFORM_ENV_VAR} default — it is \"\n333\t            f\"the one place the platform is chosen\"\n334\t        )\n335\t    if bake_default is None:\n336\t        return 'docker-bake.hcl no longer declares a `variable \"PLATFORM\"` default'\n337\t    if mise_default != bake_default:\n338\t        return (\n339\t            f\"platform default drift: mise.toml says {mise_default!r} but \"\n340\t            f\"docker-bake.hcl says {bake_default!r} — CI would build one \"\n341\t            f\"architecture while the devcontainer asks for the other\"\n342\t        )\n343\t    return None\n344\t\n345\t\n346\t#: What ``dotfiles-setup platform <field>`` can print. Shell callers (the\n347\t#: `verify-arch` task) read these instead of re-implementing the triple→arch\n348\t#: and triple→uname mappings in bash ([[zero-bash-logic]]).\n349\tPLATFORM_FIELDS = (\"triple\", \"arch\", \"machine\")\n350\t\n351\t\n352\tdef platform_field(field: str, *, platform: str | None = None) -> str:\n353\t    \"\"\"One resolved platform fact, addressed by name.\n354\t\n355\t    ``triple`` is the platform itself, ``arch`` docker's architecture name and\n356\t    ``machine`` what ``uname -m`` reports inside a container of it.\n357\t    \"\"\"\n358\t    resolved = resolve_platform(platform)\n359\t    if field == \"triple\":\n360\t        return resolved\n361\t    if field == \"arch\":\n362\t        return platform_arch(resolved)\n363\t    if field == \"machine\":\n364\t        return expected_uname_machine(resolved)\n365\t    msg = f\"unknown platform field {field!r}: expected one of {PLATFORM_FIELDS}\"\n366\t    raise ValueError(msg)\n367\t\n368\t\n369\tdef platform_main(field: str) -> int:\n370\t    \"\"\"CLI entry: print one resolved platform fact on stdout.\"\"\"\n371\t    sys.stdout.write(f\"{platform_field(field)}\\n\")\n372\t    return 0\n373\t\n374\t\n375\tdef platform_literals_main(repo_root: Path) -> int:\n376\t    \"\"\"CLI entry: report re-appearing literals and default drift; exit 1 if any.\"\"\"\n377\t    failed = False\n378\t    drift = find_default_drift(repo_root)\n379\t    if drift is not None:\n380\t        failed = True\n381\t        logger.error(\"platform-literals FAIL: %s\", drift)\n382\t    violations = find_violations(repo_root)\n383\t    if violations:\n384\t        failed = True\n385\t        logger.error(\n386\t            \"platform-literals FAIL: %d hard-coded literal(s) outside the \"\n387\t            \"permitted defaults (%s). The platform is ONE parameter (#673): \"\n388\t            \"config resolves it from %s, Python from \"\n389\t            \"dotfiles_setup.platform_target.resolve_platform()\",\n390\t            len(violations),\n391\t            \", \".join(DEFAULT_LITERAL_SITES),\n392\t            PLATFORM_ENV_VAR,\n393\t        )\n394\t        for violation in violations:\n395\t            logger.error(\"platform-literals %s\", violation.render())\n396\t    if failed:\n397\t        return 1\n398\t    logger.info(\n399\t        \"platform-literals OK: the only platform defaults are %s, and they agree\",\n400\t        \", \".join(DEFAULT_LITERAL_SITES),\n401\t    )\n402\t    return 0\n403\t"}]},"uuid":"5430e1c2-7b86-4d74-9799-341a8775ecff","timestamp":"2026-08-10T11:35:31.607Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8b450a21-ebe8-49b5-a923-460893173f8c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/rmanaloto/dev/github/ray-manaloto/dotfiles","sessionId":"eeaf6a41-3473-4c9e-a681-cca17051c6ab","version":"2.1.226","gitBranch":"feat/677-arch-scoped-devcontainer-names"}
